## 23. Limit-Up Certainty Framework

- Source file: `/Users/cuiyong/.codex/skills/youtube-investor-distiller/references/limit-up-certainty-framework.md`

## Limit-Up Certainty Framework

This reference distills the YouTube video `十个游资九个打板，为什么游资几乎都是打板出身？`.

- URL: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9_vxNr3VxfY`
- Channel: `A股作手`
- Source access: imported into NotebookLM on 2026-06-19 via the user's logged-in browser state.
- NotebookLM limitation: the analysis is based on the YouTube text transcript imported by NotebookLM, not independent audio/video inspection.
- Use with: `beijing-chaojia-framework.md`, because this video reinforces and explains the logic underneath Beijing Chaojia-style first-board participation.

This is an educational trading-method note, not investment advice.

### One-Sentence Thesis

The core claim is that `打板` is not simply chasing the highest price. It is a short-term trader's way of paying a higher entry cost to buy a confirmed market consensus, visible liquidity, and next-day premium probability.

### Why Hot Money Traders Prefer Limit-Up Buying

The video explains `十个游资九个打板` through three kinds of uncertainty that limit-up buying tries to remove:

1. `方向不确定性`
   - Low buying can keep falling.
   - Mid-move buying can fail and reverse.
   - A sealed limit-up is treated as the clearest market statement that strong buyers have won this round.

2. `时间不确定性`
   - Short-term capital cannot tolerate long idle periods.
   - A limit-up often marks the transition into the fastest acceleration phase.
   - 打板 compresses waiting time and improves capital turnover when the pattern works.

3. `流动性不确定性`
   - Large capital cannot buy enough shares during quiet movement.
   - The limit-up area creates a concentrated exchange point between sellers taking profit and buyers seeking exposure.
   - For larger players, the board is a liquidity interface, not only a technical signal.

### Highest-Value Principles

1. 涨停 is the strongest visible signal in the short-term game.
   - The video treats limit-up as the strongest K-line expression of bullish consensus.
   - It does not mean the stock is safe; it means market attention and buying pressure are now explicit.

2. 打板 sacrifices price advantage for certainty.
   - The trader gives up the profit between early intraday price and limit-up price.
   - The trade-off is buying after the market has confirmed strength, rather than guessing before confirmation.

3. The 9.8% to 10% move is a qualitative threshold.
   - Near-limit strength is still a hypothesis.
   - A real sealed board is treated as a completed consensus event.
   - This is why the video warns against buying just because the stock is close to limit-up.

4. Volume at the board matters.
   - A valid board should have enough turnover to prove that real money is exchanging shares.
   - Thin or illiquid boards can be hard to enter, hard to exit, and easy to manipulate.

5. Limit-up has an advertising effect.
   - The board tells the whole market where today's attention is.
   - The best boards become tomorrow's watchlist for momentum traders.

6. 打板 must be paired with theme logic.
   - A random board has weak continuation value.
   - A board inside the strongest theme, especially the leading or most recognizable stock, has better premium potential.

7. The target is next-day premium, not today's cheapness.
   - The reason to buy at the high price is the expectation of emotional premium the next day.
   - If no premium appears, the trade thesis is weakened quickly.

8. 打板 is a simplification mechanism.
   - It turns a huge stock universe into a smaller set of visible, high-attention candidates.
   - This is useful only if the trader still filters for theme, volume, rank, and market temperature.

9. The method works best in a strong short-term ecology.
   - More涨停, higher premium rates, and stronger repair after failed boards improve the method's environment.
   - In weak markets, blind board chasing becomes a high-frequency loss machine.

10. Buying high is not the risk; buying unconfirmed or low-quality strength is the risk.
    - The video's logic is that a confirmed board can be safer than an apparently cheaper but unconfirmed intraday move.
    - This only holds when the board is real, liquid, thematic, and supported by market temperature.

### Trading System Skeleton

#### Market Ecology

Positive environment:

- Many limit-up stocks.
- Strong short-term themes.
- Clear leading stocks.
- Next-day premiums exist.
- Failed boards can repair.
- Market is willing to reward acceleration.

Negative environment:

- Few limit-up stocks.
- Frequent failed boards.
- Leaders cannot continue.
- Next-day low opens are common.
- Theme rotation is too fast.
- Index or short-term sentiment is weak.

#### Stock Filtering

Positive filters:

- Current strong theme or sector.
- Leading stock or highly recognizable core stock.
- Early or decisive board compared with same-theme peers.
- Enough turnover and liquidity at the board.
- Board has a clear narrative, not just random price movement.
- Strong advertising effect: other traders will notice it immediately.

Negative filters:

- Non-mainstream, low-recognition board.
- No theme logic.
- Weak or late follower.
- Thin liquidity.
- Board near close only because of noise.
- Repeated failed boards in the same market environment.
- Buying at 9% or 9.8% only because of fear of missing the board.

#### Entry

Core entry idea:

- Buy only after the board is confirmed or at the moment the last sell order is being eaten and the board is clearly sealing.
- The trade is not "it rose a lot, so buy"; the trade is "the market has revealed a consensus event with liquidity."

Operational guardrails:

- Do not pre-buy merely because the price is close to涨停.
- Do not buy a board with no theme or recognition.
- Do not buy if market temperature does not support next-day premium.
- Do not treat every封板 as equivalent; rank it by theme strength, order of board, volume, and recognition.

#### Exit

Primary expected edge:

- Next-day premium or continuation.

If the next day is strong:

- Follow predefined profit-taking or continuation rules.
- Watch whether the stock keeps theme-leader status.

If the next day is weak:

- Low open and weak follow-through means the expected premium failed.
- Handle quickly rather than turning a short-term board trade into a passive hold.

#### Position Management

The transcript did not give a detailed sizing formula. Use the Beijing Chaojia risk-control layer:

- Use small size while practicing.
- Reduce size after consecutive losses.
- Trade fewer names in weak markets.
- Do not increase size merely because a board "looks certain."
- Never let the philosophical idea of certainty become permission for reckless concentration.

#### Loss Handling

If a board fails:

- First classify whether the error was market environment, stock selection, timing, or execution.
- If it was bought before confirmation, mark it as mode drift.
- If it was not a theme leader, mark it as recognition error.
- If market premium rate was weak, mark it as cycle mismatch.
- After repeated failed-board losses, reduce size or stop trading this mode temporarily.

### Copyable Rules

1. `No board, no board-trade.`
   - If the system is 打板, near-limit is not enough.

2. `No theme, no board.`
   - A random涨停 is a signal without audience.

3. `No volume, no participation.`
   - The board must show real exchange, not only a decorative price mark.

4. `Buy premium probability, not cheapness.`
   - The purpose is next-day emotional premium, not intraday bargain hunting.

5. `Rank boards before buying.`
   - Prefer the strongest theme, the earliest or clearest board, and the most recognizable stock.

6. `Failed premium equals failed thesis.`
   - If the next day does not confirm premium and momentum, do not rationalize endlessly.

7. `Market temperature is part of the setup.`
   - The same board has different odds in a hot short-term market versus a cold market.

### Beginner Checklist

Before buying:

1. Is this stock in today's strongest theme?
2. Is it a leader or high-recognition name, not a random follower?
3. Did it truly seal the board, or am I guessing at 9%+?
4. Is the board supported by enough volume?
5. Are same-theme peers also strong?
6. Does today's market have enough涨停 and premium expectation?
7. If it opens weak tomorrow, what exact exit behavior will I follow?
8. Is my position size small enough to execute the exit without emotional paralysis?

After selling:

1. Did I buy a confirmed board or a near-board guess?
2. Was the failure caused by weak market temperature?
3. Was the stock a true leader?
4. Did the board have real liquidity?
5. Did I follow the next-day plan?

### Common Misuses

- Treating every涨停 as a valid buy signal.
- Buying at 9% because of fear, then calling it 打板.
- Ignoring theme rank and recognition.
- Ignoring market temperature.
- Over-sizing because the word "certainty" sounds safe.
- Holding a failed premium trade as if it became a medium-term investment.
- Copying hot-money tactics without hot-money speed, capital structure, and execution discipline.

### Copyable vs Hard To Copy

Copyable:

- Understanding limit-up as a confirmation filter.
- Waiting for real board confirmation.
- Filtering by theme, recognition, and volume.
- Reviewing next-day premium.
- Reducing or stopping when the cycle is cold.

Hard to copy:

- Instant judgment of order-book strength.
- Speed at the sealing moment.
- Ability to influence a board with capital.
- Deep memory of all active A-share names.
- Emotional tolerance for frequent small losses and rapid exits.
- Non-public information or private network advantages.

### Relationship To Beijing Chaojia Framework

Reinforced:

- 涨停板 as a certainty filter.
- First-board or board-moment participation.
- Core/recognition filtering.
- Mechanical next-day handling.
- Avoiding low-quality opportunities.

Added:

- The liquidity-interface explanation for larger capital.
- The 9.8% to 10% qualitative threshold.
- The advertising effect of limit-up boards.
- The explicit trade-off: sacrifice price advantage for confirmed premium probability.

Potential tension:

- This video talks philosophically about certainty and may sound like high-conviction board buying.
- Beijing Chaojia's framework adds more survival constraints: negative filters, small position practice, mechanical exits, and size reduction after losses.
- For the user's trading manual, treat Beijing Chaojia's risk controls as the wrapper around this video's board logic.

### Trading Manual Entries

1. A limit-up board is a consensus event, not a standalone buy command.
2. 打板 means paying for confirmation; do not pay that price without theme, rank, volume, and market temperature.
3. The difference between 9.8% and 10% is not 0.2%; it is the difference between hypothesis and completed consensus.
4. The expected profit source is next-day premium. If premium fails, the thesis is impaired.
5. In weak markets, the same board signal should be discounted or ignored.
6. Use Beijing Chaojia-style position control when practicing: small size, predefined exits, size reduction after losses.

### Review Questions

1. Was today's market temperature suitable for 打板?
2. Was this board inside the strongest theme?
3. Was this stock a leader or just a follower?
4. Did I buy after confirmation, or did I anticipate near the board?
5. Was turnover large enough to prove real liquidity?
6. Did the board create visible market advertising effect?
7. What next-day premium did I expect, and did it appear?
8. If the next day opened weak, did I exit according to plan?
9. Was my loss due to selection, timing, market cycle, or execution?
10. Did I confuse "certainty filter" with "risk-free trade"?

### Future Distillation Targets

When future videos discuss 打板, check whether they add concrete detail on:

- Order-book strength at sealing moment.
- Opening auction rules after a board.
- Failed-board exit rules.
- First board versus second board versus high board.
- How to rank leaders within the same theme.
- Premium-rate thresholds.
- Liquidity and turnover minimums.
- How small accounts should simplify execution.

## 24. Qianniuge Incremental Capital Cycle Framework

- Source file: `/Users/cuiyong/.codex/skills/youtube-investor-distiller/references/qianniuge-incremental-capital-cycle-framework.md`

## Qianniuge Incremental Capital Cycle Framework

This reference distills the YouTube video `不训人只上硬货！游资大佬牵牛哥拆解周期核心，增量资金藏着暴富密码|大胡子学炒股`.

- URL: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QYS-W_4mMAE`
- Channel: `A股作手`
- Core figure identified by NotebookLM transcript: `牵牛哥` / `Qianniu Ge`
- Host / counterparty: `大胡子`
- Source access: imported into NotebookLM on 2026-06-19 via the user's logged-in browser state.
- NotebookLM limitation: the analysis is based on the YouTube text transcript imported by NotebookLM, not independent audio/video inspection.

This is an educational trading-method note, not investment advice.

### One-Sentence Thesis

牵牛哥的核心框架是：先用`大盘成交量台阶`给市场周期定性，再用`增量资金属性`判断钱来自公募、量化、两融还是游资，最后只在具备`逻辑延展性`和持续量能的主流板块里交易；逻辑坏了或量能连续衰退，就退出，不在非主流里补仓求回本。

### Highest-Value Principles

1. 周期不是口号，而是资金能级切换。
   - 新周期的起点通常伴随成交量从旧平台跃迁到新平台。
   - Without incremental capital, most themes are only rotation noise.

2. 主流板块需要两个条件：`逻辑延展性` plus `资金增量`.
   - 逻辑提供想象空间和持仓理由。
   - 资金增量提供价格推动力和波动空间。

3. 成交量是周期定性的第一证据。
   - Example from transcript: a May 29 cycle was identified because market volume jumped from a lower range to above one trillion and stayed there.
   - The exact threshold changes by market, but the method is stable: look for a durable volume step, not one-day excitement.

4. Different capital types create different markets.
   - 公募 / mutual funds favor value-growth directions with institutional capacity.
   - 私募量化 favors thematic rotation and momentum.
   - 两融 / margin money often reflects late retail emotion and can lag the real start.
   - 游资 favors short-term consensus and emotional acceleration.

5. 逻辑越`虚`, opportunity may be earlier.
   - When only a few people understand a new thesis, there can be expectation gap.
   - When the logic is everywhere on public media, the trade may be near late stage.

6. 量化 compresses time.
   - In a quant-heavy environment, price discovery can happen much faster.
   - What once took two days can be compressed into one day because models chase language, heat, and momentum.

7. Quant's strength is also its weakness.
   - Quant funds can accelerate a theme while liquidity supports them.
   - When strategies become crowded or market volume cannot carry them, the same feedback loop can accelerate collapse.

8. Trade inside the mainstream's `breathing`.
   - Pullbacks inside the real mainstream can be normal breathing.
   - Pullbacks in non-mainstream names are often trend failure.
   - The same red candle means different things depending on whether the stock is in the main current.

9. 板块三日缩量 is a major warning.
   - If a hot sector's volume contracts for several days from high level, its cycle may be ending.
   - Do not keep holding only because the story still sounds good.

10. Non-mainstream loss cannot be solved by averaging down.
    - If a losing position is outside the current mainstream, adding money usually compounds the error.
    - First classify whether the thesis is still mainstream and alive.

11. The trader's job is not to recover face.
    - `想回本` thinking distorts cycle judgment.
    - The correct question is whether the current capital logic still exists.

### Market Cycle Framework

#### Step 1: Market Volume Staircase

Classify the broad market:

1. `缩量旧周期`
   - Market volume is low or falling.
   - Most breakouts lack durability.
   - Cash or light testing is favored.

2. `量能阶跃`
   - Market turnover rises to a new band and holds for several days.
   - This suggests a new capital source or renewed risk appetite.
   - Begin searching for the new mainstream.

3. `增量确认`
   - Volume remains high.
   - Several sectors compete, but one or two attract sustained inflow.
   - Position can increase only inside confirmed mainstream lines.

4. `高位衰退`
   - Mainstream sector volume shrinks from high level.
   - Public attention becomes saturated.
   - Watch for three-day contraction, new competing themes, and margin enthusiasm.

Manual rule:

- Do not call a new cycle only because the index rises one day.
- Require a volume step plus a coherent capital destination.

#### Step 2: Identify Capital Type

##### Public Funds / 公募

Typical preference:

- Value-growth.
- High-capacity sectors.
- Names with institutional liquidity.
- Examples from NotebookLM transcript: CPO and innovative drugs.

Signals:

- Fund issuance or subscription improves.
- Institutional sectors receive sustained inflow.
- Trend is smoother than pure hot-money boards.
- Pullbacks may be emotional rather than logic-breaking.

##### Private Quant / 私募量化

Typical preference:

- Thematic rotation.
- Momentum.
- High news density / `语料`.
- Fast price confirmation.

Signals:

- Sudden volume burst in news-heavy themes.
- Multiple small and mid-cap names move quickly.
- Theme changes fast when language heat changes.
- Price movement may be faster than fundamental digestion.

##### Margin / 两融

Typical preference:

- Often follows visible performance.
- Represents delayed retail confidence.

Signals:

- If market rallies but margin balance does not rise, the move may be institution-led rather than retail-led.
- If margin money surges late after a major rise, beware emotional overheating.

##### Hot Money / 游资

Typical preference:

- Emotional leaders.
- Limit-up consensus.
- Short-term theme acceleration.

Signals:

- Board premium improves.
- Dragon leaders drive followers.
- Market has short-term money-making effect.

#### Step 3: Find The Mainstream

A mainstream sector should have:

- Durable volume expansion.
- A logic that can extend into new branches.
- Market recognition, but not full public saturation.
- Ability to attract a specific capital type.
- Stronger asset comparison than competing themes.

Avoid:

- A single stock without sector inflow.
- A fully public story after major gains.
- A theme with volume contraction despite good narratives.
- A trade that relies on the trader explaining why the market "should" care.

### Logical Extensibility

`逻辑延展性` means a theme can keep generating new reasons for funds to stay.

Examples of extension:

- Main line expands to upstream / downstream / adjacent links.
- A new policy, order, performance, or industry catalyst deepens the thesis.
- A sector creates new branches, such as a core technology spreading to components, materials, applications, or infrastructure.
- Sell-side and buy-side discussion evolves rather than repeats the same old slogan.

Warning signs:

- Everyone is repeating the same surface-level phrase.
- Public-media heat is high but sector volume weakens.
- No new branch can attract fresh money.
- The logic moves from `虚` expectation gap to `实` consensus saturation.

Manual test:

1. What was the original thesis?
2. What new branch appeared today?
3. Did money follow that branch?
4. Is the market learning something new, or only repeating old news?

### Trading System

#### Environment Rules

Positive environment:

- Broad-market volume steps up and holds.
- Mainstream sector volume expands or remains high.
- The capital type behind the move is identifiable.
- The sector thesis keeps extending.
- Pullbacks are bought and do not break the logic.

Negative environment:

- Broad market volume fades.
- Hot sector volume contracts for several days.
- Margin enthusiasm arrives late after a long rise.
- The only reason to hold is hope of recovery.
- New themes beat the old mainstream in asset comparison.

#### Sector Selection

Positive filters:

- Mainstream with measurable inflow.
- High logical extensibility.
- Fits the dominant capital style.
- Sector volume does not scatter.
- Has institutional capacity or hot-money emotional recognition, depending on the cycle.

Negative filters:

- Non-mainstream.
- Only one-day news.
- No durable volume.
- Logic fully saturated in public discourse.
- Volume high only because of final frenzy.

#### Stock / Instrument Selection

For public-fund cycles:

- Prefer liquid value-growth leaders or sector ETFs.
- Avoid thin-liquidity names that institutions cannot buy meaningfully.

For quant/thematic cycles:

- Prefer names with strong volume, news density, and momentum.
- Keep holding periods shorter because rotation can be fast.

For hot-money cycles:

- Use leader / ladder / premium rules from the dragon framework.
- Do not confuse a broad incremental-capital wave with a pure board strategy.

#### Entry

Allowed entries:

1. `量能阶跃确认`
   - Market volume steps up and mainstream sector appears.
   - Enter core sector instruments or leaders after confirmation.

2. `主流分歧呼吸`
   - Sector logic remains intact but price pulls back emotionally.
   - Buy only inside mainstream; do not use this for non-mainstream names.

3. `逻辑延展加仓`
   - A new branch deepens the thesis and volume follows.
   - Add only when extension is confirmed by money.

4. `量化动量试错`
   - News density and volume surge together.
   - Use smaller size or faster exit because rotation speed is high.

#### Exit

Exit or reduce when:

- Sector volume contracts from high level for about three days.
- Core logic stops extending.
- New competing theme wins asset comparison.
- Public media saturation becomes obvious after large gains.
- Margin money surges late and emotion overheats.
- Position is outside current mainstream and losing.
- The trader is tempted to average down for recovery.

#### Position Management

Principles:

- Size follows cycle clarity and capital type.
- Incremental confirmed cycles can justify higher exposure, but only in mainstream.
- Mixed or chaotic markets require reduced size.
- Quant-driven themes need stricter holding and exit discipline.

Beginner guardrail:

- Treat full-position language as expert-level context, not default permission.
- Use partial positions until the framework has been verified in personal review.

#### Loss Handling

After a loss, classify:

1. Was the market cycle misread?
2. Was the capital type misidentified?
3. Did sector volume deteriorate before entry?
4. Was the stock outside the mainstream?
5. Did I average down because I wanted to recover?

Rule:

- Logic failure is an exit signal, not a discount.

### Copyable Rules

1. `先定周期，再选板块。`
   - No cycle classification, no meaningful trade plan.

2. `量能台阶确认新周期。`
   - Require durable market-volume step-up, not one-day index excitement.

3. `主流 = 逻辑延展性 + 资金增量。`
   - Story alone is not enough; money alone without logic is unstable.

4. `先识别资金属性。`
   - 公募, 量化, 两融, and 游资 create different patterns.

5. `逻辑越虚越早，逻辑全网皆知越晚。`
   - Expectation gap is early; public saturation is dangerous.

6. `板块三日缩量要警惕。`
   - High-level volume contraction often means the cycle is ending.

7. `只在主流里呼吸。`
   - Pullback buying works only when mainstream logic remains intact.

8. `非主流亏损不补仓。`
   - Adding to a losing non-mainstream position compounds wrongness.

9. `量化题材快进快出。`
   - Quant momentum can accelerate both rise and collapse.

10. `不要为了回本交易。`
    - Recovery emotion corrupts cycle judgment.

### Beginner Checklist

Before buying:

1. Is market volume in an old band, new step-up, confirmed increment, or high-level fade?
2. What is the current mainstream sector?
3. Which capital type is driving it: public fund, quant, margin, hot money, or mixed?
4. What is the sector's logical extension today?
5. Did volume confirm that extension?
6. Is the thesis still early / `虚`, or already saturated / `实`?
7. Is this position inside the mainstream?
8. If this falls, is it normal breathing or logic failure?
9. What condition makes me exit?
10. Am I trading a law I can explain, or trying to win back money?

After trading:

1. Did the sector volume expand or contract?
2. Did a new branch of logic appear?
3. Did the dominant capital style change?
4. Did I hold a non-mainstream loser?
5. Did I confuse a quant momentum trade with an institutional trend?

### Common Misuses

- Calling every volume uptick a new cycle.
- Buying one-day themes without identifying incremental capital.
- Using public-fund trend rules on quant momentum themes.
- Using quant momentum speed in slow institutional sectors.
- Treating `主流里呼吸` as permission to average down weak stocks.
- Ignoring sector volume because the story still sounds exciting.
- Buying when the logic is already everywhere.
- Over-focusing on small tactical formulas like one-to-two patterns while ignoring cycle and capital source.
- Taking examples such as CPO or innovative drugs as recommendations rather than examples of the method.
- Waiting for two-margin data to confirm too late, then entering near emotional saturation.

### Copyable vs Hard To Copy

Copyable:

- Market-volume staircase.
- Capital-type classification.
- Sector-volume tracking.
- Logical-extension checklist.
- Three-day sector-volume contraction warning.
- Non-mainstream no-averaging rule.
- Public saturation warning.

Hard to copy:

- Access to high-quality sell-side or industry interpretation.
- Fast recognition of a real logic extension before it becomes public.
- Macro synthesis across liquidity, policy, and industry cycles.
- Feeling the mainstream's `breathing` in real time.
- Knowing when quant speed is still supportive versus already crowded.

### Relationship To Existing Frameworks

#### Baozong Institutional Trend

Reinforced:

- Institution-led phases require different tactics from pure hot-money boards.
- High-liquidity value-growth sectors and ETFs may be superior when institutions dominate.
- Pullbacks are buyable only when logic remains intact.

Added by Qianniuge:

- Broader capital-source taxonomy: public funds, quant, margin, hot money.
- Market-volume staircase as cycle definition.
- Logic extension and public saturation as timing tools.

Manual synthesis:

- Use Qianniuge to identify whether the capital wave exists and who drives it.
- Use Baozong to trade institution-led value-growth waves with trend and breathing rules.

#### Chaogu Yangjia

Reinforced:

- Mainstream and crowd recognition matter.
- Do not be controlled by the desire to recover cost.
- Market ecology comes before individual stock.

Added:

- More explicit quant and public-fund mechanics.
- `逻辑虚实` as a way to define expectation gap.

#### Yaotouge

Reinforced:

- Cycle state comes before setup.
- Weak or chaotic markets demand lower frequency.

Difference:

- Yaotouge reads high-level leader feedback and board ecology.
- Qianniuge reads broad-market volume and capital-source structure.

Manual synthesis:

- Use Qianniuge for top-down cycle and capital source.
- Use Yaotouge for short-term board-role execution inside hot-money cycles.

#### Dragon Leader Strategy

Reinforced:

- A leader needs market consensus and sector support.
- Isolated K-lines are not enough.

Potential tension:

- Pure ladder rules can be too tactical if the market's dominant capital is institutional or quant.
- Qianniuge warns against worshiping small formulas while missing the bigger capital cycle.

Manual synthesis:

- Use the dragon ladder only after confirming the capital style rewards hot-money leader behavior.

#### Youzi Growth Common Framework

Reinforced:

- 主流 first, individual stock second.
- Volume is the battlefield.
- Plan and review beat intraday impulse.

Added:

- Capital-source classification.
- Public saturation and `逻辑生化` as additional filters.

### Trading Manual Entries

1. Before trading, classify broad-market volume: old band, step-up, confirmed increment, or high-level fade.
2. Identify the dominant capital type: public fund, quant, margin, hot money, or mixed.
3. Define the current mainstream sector by both logic extension and capital inflow.
4. If market volume steps up and sector volume follows for several days, treat the cycle as tradable.
5. If sector logic extends into new branches and money confirms, hold or add inside the mainstream.
6. If sector volume shrinks from high level for about three days, reduce or exit.
7. If a position is not in the current mainstream and is losing, stop; do not average down.
8. If public attention becomes saturated after a major rise, stop trying to earn the last part.
9. In quant-driven themes, shorten holding windows and respect volume deterioration quickly.
10. In institution-led value-growth themes, use Baozong-style breathing and ETF alternatives.

### Review Questions

1. What volume band is the broad market in today?
2. Did market volume create or maintain a new step?
3. Which sector received real incremental capital?
4. What type of capital is driving the sector?
5. What is the sector's new logical extension today?
6. Is the thesis still early and under-discussed, or already public and crowded?
7. Did sector volume expand, stay high, or shrink?
8. Is my trade inside the mainstream's breathing, or outside the mainstream hoping for recovery?
9. Did margin data indicate late retail enthusiasm?
10. If the logic is wrong, what is my immediate exit?

### Future Distillation Targets

When future videos mention 牵牛哥, 千牛哥, 增量资金, or周期核心, look for:

- Exact thresholds for market-volume staircase by market phase.
- How he reads two-margin data in real time.
- Concrete examples of `逻辑生化`.
- How to distinguish public-fund trend from quant momentum.
- Whether the three-day sector-volume warning has exceptions.
- How he sizes positions during mixed public-fund and quant markets.

## 25. Sangtianlu Quant Low-Frequency Window Framework

## Sangtianlu Quant Low-Frequency Window Framework

This reference distills the YouTube video `顶级游资桑田路宝总连线大胡子|大胡子学炒股`.

- URL: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Lu9-JlkP8Vk`
- Channel shown by YouTube metadata / NotebookLM transcript: `A股作手`
- Core figures identified by NotebookLM transcript: `桑田路 / 宝总` as the senior trader; `大胡子` as the livestream host / learner.
- Source access: imported into NotebookLM on 2026-06-20 via the user's logged-in browser state.
- NotebookLM limitation: this analysis is based on the YouTube text transcript imported by NotebookLM, not independent audio/video inspection. The YouTube page metadata exposed the title and description, but no public caption tracks were visible through the page object.
- NotebookLM import status: successful `1 source`; notebook title: `Trading Strategy and Livestreaming Insights with Master Sangtianlu`.

This is an educational trading-method note, not investment advice.

### One-Sentence Thesis

桑田路 / 宝总这条连线的核心不是再教一个高频短线技巧，而是给出`量化主导市场下的主观交易者生存法`: 承认日内小波动已被量化用速度和胜率烫平，普通主观交易者应强制降频，只在`大行情`、`大题材`、`大恐慌空翻多`三类高赔率窗口进攻，其他时间把保护本金、晚间复盘和少做无效交易视为真正的交易能力。

### Confirmed Parameters And Limits

These are parameters NotebookLM extracted as explicit or context-confirmed from the transcript. Do not overfit them beyond the stated meaning.

| Confirmed Parameter | Scene | Executable Meaning | Do Not Overextend |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `晚上11点` | 每日复盘 | Deep review should be completed by around 23:00; senior traders still do daily homework late at night. | Not a mandated start time; the transcript does not define a full复盘模板. |
| `11个ST / 十几个票` | 风险环境判断 | A day with about 11 ST / more than ten problematic names is treated as obviously恶劣 and demands next-day caution. | Not a universal mechanical threshold; use it as a risk量级 sample. |
| `3种盈利窗口` | 全年交易准入 | Only three windows deserve aggressive participation: 大行情, 大题材, 大恐慌. | No exact成交额,跌幅, or breadth threshold was given. |
| `1秒钟 vs 几小时` | 量化速度优势 | In intraday micro-moves, quant can complete in seconds what discretionary traders analyze slowly; avoid competing at that level. | Treat as a speed metaphor, not a stopwatch rule. |
| `100股 / 约1000元` | 实盘门槛类比 | Stock trading has a minimum physical participation threshold, unlike content traffic. | Not a recommended account size or expected profit base. |
| `1万人以上` | 直播流量 / 强一致性类比 | Extreme visibility triggers monitoring and crowd risk; in stocks, over-obvious consensus can also become dangerous. | This was originally about livestreaming; use as an analogy to crowded consensus, not a stock-screening number. |
| `低频` | 交易频率 | In a quant-heavy market, reduce trading frequency sharply. | The transcript does not give a daily trade-count cap. |
| `价格中枢` | 网格 / 做T | Sideways markets with a stable center may allow grid-like high-sell-low-buy actions. | Once trend or major theme starts, grid logic can fail. |

No clear fixed stop-loss percentage, fixed仓位比例, or exact大恐慌 percentage threshold was confirmed by the transcript.

### Highest-Value Principles

1. `量化已经改变短线生态`.
   - Quant funds dominate speed, short-horizon reaction, order placement, and small-spread arbitrage.
   - Old discretionary intraday logic that relied on slow human reaction is increasingly weak.

2. `游资话语权下降`.
   - Traditional hot-money influence and龙虎榜 narrative power are weaker when quant funds are the dominant participant.
   - This does not mean hot money is dead; it means old playbooks cannot be copied mechanically.

3. `低频是主观交易者的防守武器`.
   - If the opponent wins on speed and frequency, the answer is not to click faster.
   - Reduce frequency, wait for windows where human judgment and patience matter more than millisecond execution.

4. `一年真正值得进攻的是三种窗口`.
   - 大行情: broad-market incremental capital.
   - 大题材: local incremental capital inside a major theme.
   - 大恐慌: panic selling creates empty-to-long / short-covering style rebound energy.

5. `平庸时间是受苦受难期`.
   - Most days are not fertile ground.
   - Surviving flat, choppy, quant-smoothed markets is a positive achievement.

6. `量化胜率高, 但赔率低`.
   - Quant can harvest frequent small edges.
   - Its weakness is lower格局: in high-volatility, high-payoff windows, it may exit too early or crowd the same short-term edge.

7. `强一致性机会可能是陷阱`.
   - If everyone can see the same board / theme and quant orders are already queued, the trade may look certain but offer poor next-day odds.
   - "看懂了" and "买得到" can become mutually exclusive.

8. `大恐慌不是小反抽`.
   - The model wants panic severe enough to create空翻多增量.
   - Buying too early in normal weakness is catching a falling knife, not exploiting panic reversal.

9. `晚上复盘是风险边界工作`.
   - The transcript highlights late-night review and ST / problem-stock counts.
   - Review is not decoration; it defines next-day caution or participation.

10. `心力和生活状态 affect trading survival`.
    - The transcript links long-term trading with心态,热情, and resisting damage from screens, losses, and attention.
    - This is not a buy/sell signal, but it is a system-maintenance condition.

### Hard Rule Table

| Rule | Observe | Trigger | Action | Disabled / Invalid When | Common Misuse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 三大利润窗口 | Broad market, theme strength, panic intensity | 大行情 / 大题材 / 大恐慌 appears | Allow active or heavy participation according to personal risk plan | None of the three windows exist | Calling a small bounce a big panic; calling one-day rotation a big theme |
| 强制降频 | Personal trade log, costs, churn | Frequent small wins followed by a larger loss; net effect resembles working for brokers | Stop or reduce to very low frequency | A confirmed big window appears | Mistaking busyness for盘感 |
| 避开量化卡位 | Order book, limit-up queue, obvious consensus | Strong consensus board or one-line board already filled with fast money / quant queue | Give up chasing or queuing | Broad incremental bull market where crowding is absorbed | Treating crowded queue as safety |
| 大题材局部增量 | Sector heat, market recognition, theme breadth | A theme attracts most attention and creates tradable incremental flow even in a存量 market | Participate in the theme's main opportunity set | Theme is only rotation noise with no real inflow | Over-trading every news item as a "big theme" |
| 大恐慌空翻多 | Decline list, limit-down / ST / problem-stock pressure, panic feel | Panic sells off hard enough to create potential reversal energy | Consider small/progressive buying after exhaustion and reversal signs | Slow grind-down without panic exhaustion | Buying early because price looks cheap |
| 晚间11点复盘 | Daily review routine | Post-market / late-night review completed | Check risk events, ST/problem-stock count, tomorrow's window classification | None | Treating review as content consumption instead of action preparation |
| ST/暴雷风险预警 | ST / problem-stock count, major negative list | Around 11 ST / more than ten problematic names appeared in the observed example | Treat the environment as恶劣 and be cautious the next day | Normal dispersion without systemic warning | Turning "11" into a permanent magic threshold |
| 网格震荡 | Price center / range behavior | Quant-smoothed sideways market with stable中枢 | Only skilled traders may do controlled grid / 做T | Trend starts, big theme starts, panic breaks range | Using grid in a trend and selling winners too early |
| 利用量化无格局 | Big theme or big market with high payoff | Quant may exit early due to high win-rate / low-payoff design | Let valid high-payoff position breathe if the big window remains alive | No big window; thesis deteriorates | Using "格局" to justify holding weak positions |
| 保护自己 | Account drawdown, mental state, bad ecology | Market is not in a valid window or personal state is poor | Rest, reduce frequency, reduce risk | A valid window plus prepared plan | Using "rest" as avoidance after valid signals |

### Trading System

#### Market Ecology

Primary classification:

1. `量化主导震荡市`
   - Price moves around a stable center.
   - Intraday edges are quickly arbitraged away.
   - Default action: low frequency, avoid chasing, only advanced controlled grid if rules are clear.

2. `大行情`
   - Broad incremental capital enters.
   - Many participants can make money, but skilled traders should seek better payoff than the crowd.
   - Default action: increase participation only after confirming real increment.

3. `大题材`
   - A major theme creates局部增量 even when the broad market is not strong.
   - Default action: focus on the theme's core opportunity, not every related stock.

4. `大恐慌`
   - Severe selling creates a possible空翻多 window.
   - Default action: wait for exhaustion / reversal evidence, then consider cautious or progressive participation.

5. `受苦受难期`
   - None of the three profit windows exists.
   - Default action: rest, review, and protect account / mind.

#### Stock / Theme Filtering

Positive filters:

- Belongs to a genuine大题材 with local incremental capital.
- Is not merely an over-crowded one-line consensus board.
- Offers payoff that is not already consumed by quant speed and queue priority.
- Has enough liquidity but is not just a small intraday quant edge.
- In panic, appears after forced selling and reversal energy, not merely during early decline.

Negative filters:

- Strong consensus board where the trader can only queue behind fast money.
- Random intraday volatility in a quant-smoothed market.
- News-driven one-day rotation without local increment.
- Weak stock bought only because the obvious strong stock is unavailable.
- Frequent tiny trades whose gross profits disappear after costs and mistakes.

#### Entry

Allowed entries:

1. Big-market entry:
   - IF broad incremental capital is present,
   - THEN participate in the strongest route that still offers payoff.

2. Big-theme entry:
   - IF a major theme creates局部增量,
   - THEN trade the theme's best opportunity set rather than scattered followers.

3. Big-panic entry:
   - IF the decline reaches panic and begins to show空翻多 evidence,
   - THEN start with cautious / staged participation.

4. Controlled grid:
   - IF market is stable around a clear price center and the trader has sufficient skill,
   - THEN use limited grid / 做T.

Forbidden entries:

- Buying because the day is boring.
- Chasing a board only because everyone understands it.
- Competing with quant on second-by-second micro fluctuation.
- Calling every drop a panic.

#### Exit / Defense

Exit or stand down when:

- The market is outside the three profit windows.
- The trade becomes a frequency/cost churn.
- A strong-consensus trade is only available after quant has occupied queue priority.
- ST / problem-stock data suggests a sharply worsening environment.
- A grid trade loses its price center because trend or panic breaks the range.
- Personal mental state is degraded enough to cause impulsive trades.

### Quant-Adversarial Manual

#### Avoid Quant Strength

- Do not fight quant speed.
- Do not build a system dependent on being faster than algorithms.
- Do not chase obvious intraday moves that quant can complete before manual traders.
- Do not treat strong consensus boards as safe just because they are easy to understand.

#### Exploit Quant Weakness

- Look for higher-payoff windows where holding through a larger move matters.
- In major themes, allow valid winners room if the theme remains alive.
- In panic, look for quant/crowd selling that pushes price away from the likely short-term center.
- Use patience and selectivity as the "edge", not click frequency.

#### Practical If/Then

- IF the trade depends on seconds, THEN skip.
- IF the opportunity is obvious but impossible to buy except behind quant queues, THEN skip or reduce expectation.
- IF a real big theme appears and quant exits early, THEN do not sell only because a small profit is available.
- IF panic is still spreading without reversal evidence, THEN wait.
- IF panic starts reversing and creates空翻多 increment, THEN consider staged participation.

### Daily Execution Flow

#### 盘前 / 前一晚

1. Complete post-market review around the late-night window, using 23:00 as the discipline anchor.
2. Check:
   - ST / problem-stock count,
   - major negative events,
   - whether tomorrow may be more恶劣,
   - whether a big theme or broad increment exists.
3. Classify tomorrow:
   - 大行情,
   - 大题材,
   - 大恐慌,
   - 受苦受难期.

#### 早盘

1. Watch whether the market confirms the prior classification.
2. If strong-consensus boards are already one-line / quant-crowded, do not chase.
3. If panic opens, observe whether it is true panic or only normal weakness.

#### 盘中

1. If no profit window appears, keep frequency low or stay flat.
2. If a big theme appears, focus on the theme's real core rather than random followers.
3. If panic reverses into空翻多, participate carefully.
4. If using grid in quant-smoothed ranges, keep it mode-labeled and stop when range breaks.

#### 尾盘

1. Confirm whether the day's logic still belongs to one of the three windows.
2. Reduce or exit trades that were only intraday noise.
3. Avoid carrying accidental exposure from a failed high-frequency attempt.

#### 盘后

1. Audit all trades:
   - Was this in one of the three windows?
   - Did costs and churn consume the edge?
   - Was I fighting quant speed?
2. Update next-day risk:
   - ST/problem names,
   - broad increment,
   - big theme continuation,
   - panic/reversal status.

### Copyable vs Hard To Copy

Copyable:

- Classify each day into one of the three profit windows or rest.
- Reduce frequency in quant-smoothed markets.
- Avoid strong-consensus boards crowded by quant queues.
- Use late-night review to define next-day risk.
- Track whether personal trades are just cost churn.
- Treat ST/problem-stock clusters as environment warnings.

Hard to copy:

- Sensing whether panic has truly exhausted.
- Knowing when a big theme has enough local increment.
- Holding through a high-payoff window without selling too early.
- Top hot-money resource position and flow perception.
- Extreme patience during long boring periods.

### Common Misuse

1. `低频` becomes laziness.
   - Low frequency still requires daily review and preparation.

2. `大恐慌` becomes early bottom fishing.
   - The system wants panic exhaustion and空翻多, not blind cheapness.

3. `大题材` becomes every news headline.
   - A big theme must create local incremental flow, not just media excitement.

4. `量化无格局` becomes stubborn holding.
   - Holding longer is only valid inside a real high-payoff window.

5. `11个ST` becomes a magic number.
   - It is a confirmed sample from the transcript, not a universal formula.

6. `网格` is used in trends.
   - Grid only fits stable-center chop; it fails when the market transitions.

7. `保护自己` becomes refusing all opportunity.
   - Defense is default outside windows; inside true windows, the trader still needs a prepared attack plan.

### Comparison With Existing Frameworks

#### Reinforces Trader Probability Discipline

- Both emphasize frequency control and payoff over raw win rate.
- This video adds a market-ecology reason: quant can own high-frequency win-rate games, so discretionary traders must seek lower-frequency higher-payoff windows.

#### Reinforces Qianniuge Incremental Capital Cycle

- Both treat `增量` as the core of tradable opportunity.
- Qianniuge explains volume staircases and capital type; Sangtianlu adds the practical rule that大行情 and大题材 are the only times to attack aggressively.

#### Complements Baozong Institutional Trend Framework

- Baozong's institutional trend model asks which capital style is winning.
- Sangtianlu's framework asks whether the whole environment is quant-smoothed, locally incremental, or panic-reversal.
- The common lesson: stop forcing obsolete hot-money tactics when market microstructure has changed.

#### Reinforces Chaogu Yangjia Heart Method

- Both emphasize大局, emotion, and crowd effects.
- Sangtianlu is more explicit about when not to trade: most flat periods are simply not worth the damage.

#### Conflicts With Mechanical High-Frequency / Simple Board Premium Models

- The transcript directly warns against excessive frequency and simple隔日溢价 thinking.
- Traditional打板 may still work in specific windows, but strong-consensus quant-crowded boards are treated as dangerous.

#### Connects To Beijing Chaojia Risk Control

- Both value survival and avoiding bad trades.
- Sangtianlu extends risk control from individual trade selection to market-ecology selection: choose the window first, then the trade.

### Review Questions

1. Is today's market in大行情,大题材,大恐慌, or受苦受难期?
2. What evidence proves增量 exists?
3. Is this theme big enough to create local increment, or only one-day rotation?
4. Am I competing with quant on speed?
5. Is the opportunity a strong-consensus board already occupied by fast money?
6. If I skip this trade, will my account curve likely improve?
7. Did today's activity create edge, or only commission and noise?
8. Did late-night review reveal ST / problem-stock clusters?
9. Is this panic severe enough to create空翻多, or only normal weakness?
10. If using grid, is the price center still stable?
11. Am I holding for high payoff inside a real window, or just refusing to admit weakness?
12. Did my mental state make me seek action outside the three windows?

### Future Distillation Targets

When adding future videos from 桑田路 / 宝总 or related quant-era hot-money discussions, look for:

- More exact criteria for distinguishing大行情,大题材, and大恐慌.
- Observable measures of quant crowding in a board / queue.
- How to identify空翻多 confirmation without catching knives.
- Whether specific breadth, turnover, or ST/problem-stock thresholds recur.
- Concrete examples of when strong-consensus boards are still tradable.
- How to size positions differently across the three windows.
- How the framework interacts with ETF / institutional trends and hot-money dragon leaders.

## Short-Term Cycle Mode Failure Warnings Framework

This reference distills the YouTube video `为什么每次大赚之后，都会大亏？为什么在股市你总结的模式时而有效，时而无效？内含短线交易18条忠告`.

- URL: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=P2M0qFtlQhA`
- Channel: `A股作手`
- Upload date shown by YouTube metadata: `2025-01-07T05:00:34-08:00`
- Duration shown by YouTube metadata: about `1260` seconds
- Public page title: `为什么每次大赚之后，都会大亏？为什么在股市你总结的模式时而有效，时而无效？内含短线交易18条忠告`
- Captions: the browser extractor found `0` public caption tracks on the YouTube page; source text was imported through NotebookLM.
- NotebookLM title: `Mastering Stock Cycles and Short-Term Trading Logic`
- NotebookLM URL: `https://notebooklm.google.com/notebook/c2829e5d-a142-467d-9bef-cc815d1d0a6a`
- Source access: imported into NotebookLM on 2026-06-20 via the user's logged-in browser state.
- NotebookLM limitation: this note is based on NotebookLM's extraction from the YouTube source and public page metadata, not independent audio/video inspection.

This is an educational trading-method note, not investment advice.

### One-Sentence Thesis

短线交易的大赚后大亏、模式时灵时不灵，本质上不是单纯心态问题，而是交易者把周期红利误判成个人能力：赚钱效应扩张时模式被行情放大，亏钱效应放大、成交额萎缩、战法同质化、对手盘进化时，同一套模式会立刻从优势变成风险。

### Source Integrity Note

The title mentions `18条忠告`, but NotebookLM's transcript-based extraction found only `15` clearly supported short-term trading cognitions. Do not fabricate items 16-18. Treat `18` as title wording unless later transcript access proves the missing items.

### Highest-Value Principles

1. Cycle explains why the same trader alternates between large profit and large loss.
   - The market cycles between money-making effect expansion and loss-making effect expansion.
   - A method can look powerful during the profit cycle and become dangerous in the loss cycle.

2. Big profit can create the seed of the next big loss.
   - After a profitable phase, traders often mistake favorable environment for personal breakthrough.
   - They increase confidence, frequency, and size exactly when buyer exhaustion and profit-taking risk are rising.

3. Mode validity is conditional.
   - A mode is not universally effective.
   - It depends on market cycle, turnover, crowding, opponent behavior, and capital aesthetics.

4. Turnover is the lifeline of short-term trading.
   - Active turnover means active capital and higher tolerance for mistakes.
   - Shrinking turnover means fewer follow-on buyers, weaker repair ability, and more traps.
   - The source does not give a fixed market-wide turnover threshold, so this must be treated as a trend condition.

5. Correct cognition comes before execution.
   - `知行合一` only helps if the `知` is correct.
   - Persisting in a wrong cognition is just disciplined self-harm.

6. Over-popular strategies decay.
   - When a tactic becomes widely known through social media or retail education, the original information gap disappears.
   - Crowded buying can create shrinkage acceleration and then sudden reversal.

7. Be a market "slippery participant".
   - Do not be permanently bullish or bearish.
   - If the market rejects the original view, correct quickly.

8. Select `大众情人`, not private favorites.
   - A good short-term stock should satisfy multiple capital groups: emotion traders, technical traders, theme traders, and logic traders.
   - More overlapping aesthetics mean stronger follow-through potential.

9. `道` and `数` are both needed, but `道` gives the mode its lifespan.
   - `数` is the visible tactic.
   - `道` is the market logic behind when that tactic works and when it should be stopped.

10. Know the opponent.
    - Profit comes from others' mistakes.
    - The source warns against trying to harvest institutions, top hot-money players, or seasoned traders without an edge.

11. In strong markets, emotion can dominate; in weak markets, hard logic matters more.
    - When liquidity and risk appetite are high, emotional premium can push speculative names.
    - When the environment is poor, weak logic collapses faster, so harder policy/industry/theme logic matters more.

12. Loss recovery needs a process, not revenge trading.
    - After a large loss, use several days of cooling and review.
    - Rebuild confidence with `5%` or smaller position size and wait for two correct trades before normalizing size.

### Parameter Audit

|---|---|---|---|
| `18条忠告` | Video title wording | No | NotebookLM extraction found only `15` supported items |
| `15` core cognitions | Transcript-supported list of short-term principles | Yes | Treat as extracted structure, not necessarily original numbered slide count |
| `5%` or smaller position | Post-loss confidence rebuild size | Yes | Normal position size is not defined |
| `连续2笔盈利` | Condition to restore confidence after loss | Yes | Profit size of the two trades is not defined |
| `几天时间` | Cooling period after large loss | Yes as a soft rule | Natural days vs trading days is not specified |
| `持续放大/萎缩` turnover | Turnover trend used to judge capital activity | Yes as a trend filter | No absolute A-share threshold is given |
| `40%` or higher turnover | Example associated with highly active speculative names | No | Example only; not a universal buy/sell trigger |
| `13年` | Speaker/channel experience marker extracted by NotebookLM | No | Credibility context only |
| Bear market: short profit phase, long loss phase | Cycle description | Yes conceptually | No exact measurement is supplied |
| Bull market: longer profit phase, shorter but sharp loss phase | Cycle description | Yes conceptually | Still requires avoiding sudden clearing phases |

### The 15 Supported Warnings

| # | Warning | Applicable State | Trigger | Action | Reverse Mistake |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Understand the profit/loss effect cycle | All markets | Money-making or loss-making effect changes | Attack in expansion, defend in loss expansion | Forcing the same mode through all cycles |
| 2 | Step on the rhythm | Bull/bear shifts | Emotional peak, panic, or clearing phase | Avoid bull-market sharp drawdowns and bear-market false excitement | Thinking bull market means no risk |
| 3 | Cognition before execution | Learning phase | Execution cannot solve repeated losses | Stop and repair cognition first | Practicing discipline on wrong logic |
| 4 | Avoid homogeneous tactics | Strategy crowding phase | A tactic becomes widely promoted | Search for blind spots and less crowded edges | Chasing crowded shrinkage acceleration |
| 5 | Treat turnover as core | All markets | Market turnover trend changes | Trade more in active turnover, reduce in shrinkage | Forcing relay trades in low-liquidity time |
| 6 | Be slippery | Error-correction phase | Price action contradicts thesis | Admit wrong and correct | Defending bullish/bearish identity |
| 7 | Use synergy thinking | Stock selection | Multiple aesthetics overlap | Prefer public-favorite names | Buying private favorites |
| 8 | `道` over isolated `数` | Mode failure phase | Same mode becomes unstable | Study underlying logic and conditions | Searching for eternal formulas |
| 9 | Iterate fast | Long-term trading | Market feedback worsens | Update the model through review | Assuming old edge will always work |
| 10 | Pick the right opponent | Game-selection phase | Identifying who pays you | Seek situations where counterparties are weaker | Trying to harvest stronger players |
| 11 | Switch logic weight by environment | Strong/weak environment changes | Market turns hot or cold | Strong market: emotion; weak market: hard logic | Trading weak concepts in weak markets |
| 12 | Control the hand | Temptation phase | Greed, fear, or unclear market | Stop or reduce trading | Trading to satisfy action impulse |
| 13 | Board trading seeks certainty | Confirmation phase | Multiple capital paths show agreement | Use board/confirmed points only when consensus appears | Buying cold names without money recognition |
| 14 | Process selling too early or missing out | Post-exit phase | Sell-flying or missed move | Review cognition and human weakness | Emotional chase after regret |
| 15 | Repair after large loss | Severe loss phase | Large loss or consecutive losses | Cool down, review, restart with <=5% size | Trying to win it back immediately |

### Why Big Profit Is Often Followed By Big Loss

Mechanism chain:

1. Money-making effect begins.
   - Early participants make money.
   - Visible profits activate crowd greed.

2. Positive feedback expands.
   - More outside money enters.
   - The trader's mode appears highly effective.

3. The trader misattributes the gain.
   - Favorable cycle is mistaken for personal enlightenment.
   - Confidence, position size, and trading frequency increase.

4. Buying power becomes exhausted.
   - Potential buyers are already in.
   - Follow-on demand weakens.

5. Early money exits.
   - Profitable capital starts to lock in gains.
   - Price action becomes less smooth.

6. Loss-making effect begins.
   - Falling prices trigger fear and risk reduction.
   - Weak holders sell.

7. The trader keeps using the old mode.
   - The mode was adapted to the profit cycle, not the new loss cycle.
   - Large prior confidence turns into delayed stop-loss and oversized exposure.

8. Profit is returned.
   - The loss is amplified because the trader is largest and most active near the cycle turn.

Reusable guardrail:

- After a large profit phase, reduce self-trust and raise environment checks.
- Do not increase size simply because recent trades went well.

### Why A Mode Works Sometimes And Fails Other Times

#### Cycle

- In money-making effect expansion, even imperfect entries can be repaired.
- In loss-making effect expansion, good-looking entries can fail immediately.

Rule:

- Attach every mode to a market stage. If the stage changes, the mode must be revalidated.

#### Turnover

- High and expanding turnover provides follow-on liquidity.
- Shrinking turnover lowers tolerance and makes breakouts easier to fail.

Rule:

- Before using a short-term mode, check whether market-wide and sector turnover support it.

#### Homogeneity

- A mode loses information value after too many traders copy it.
- Crowding can turn the expected buy point into the exit point for stronger players.

Rule:

- If a tactic becomes a retail consensus, downgrade its expected edge.

#### Counterparty

- The trader earns from others' mistakes.
- If the main counterparties are stronger players using the same signals, the edge reverses.

Rule:

- Ask who is likely selling to me and who is likely buying after me.

#### Capital Aesthetics

- A stock needs broad market recognition.
- If only one trading style likes it, follow-through can be thin.

Rule:

- Prefer names that simultaneously satisfy theme, emotion, technical, and liquidity aesthetics.

### Trading System

#### Market Filter

Trade only after classifying:

1. Money-making effect expansion, balance, or loss-making effect expansion.
2. Turnover expansion, high-level stability, or shrinkage.
3. Strong market where emotion can work, or weak market where hard logic is required.
4. Whether current popular tactics are still useful or already crowded.

#### Stock Filter

Prefer candidates with:

- Public recognition.
- Multi-style capital appeal.
- Active turnover.
- Clear theme or hard logic depending on environment.
- A plausible weaker counterparty and stronger follow-on buyer.

Avoid:

- Private favorites.
- Low-liquidity names.
- Social-media consensus trades after the edge has become crowded.
- Weak-logic names in weak market conditions.

#### Entry

Allowed entries:

- High-certainty points where multiple capital paths show agreement.
- Board or confirmation-style entry when it represents real consensus, not mechanical chasing.
- Strong market emotional carriers when money-making effect supports them.
- Weak market hard-logic carriers when speculative emotion is unreliable.

Entry is invalid if:

- Turnover is shrinking.
- The mode is crowded and obvious.
- The trader cannot explain who buys after entry.
- The trade exists only to avoid missing out.

#### Exit

Exit or reduce when:

- Money-making effect weakens.
- Turnover shrinks.
- Follow-on buyers disappear.
- The trade thesis is rejected by price action.
- The position was entered under a mode whose environment has changed.

#### Position Management

Position follows cycle and confidence quality:

- Profit-effect expansion and active turnover: normal attack may be considered.
- Unclear or shrinking turnover: reduce size.
- Loss-effect expansion: cash or very small testing.
- After large loss: cool down for several days; restart with `5%` or less.
- Resume normal size only after at least two correct small trades and clear cycle improvement.

### Practical Rules

1. Classify the current market as money-making effect expansion, balance, or loss-making effect expansion before each trade.
2. Do not use a short-term mode during loss-making effect expansion unless the mode is specifically designed for that phase.
3. Check total market turnover before increasing size.
4. Reduce trading when turnover is shrinking.
5. Do not call a mode effective unless it has worked across multiple relevant cycles.
6. After a large profit streak, lower position aggression until the next cycle check confirms support.
7. Do not treat recent profit as proof of enlightenment.
8. Identify whether the current tactic has become too crowded.
9. Avoid trades that are obvious to everyone but no longer supported by fresh demand.
10. Prefer public-favorite stocks with multi-style capital appeal.
11. In weak markets, demand harder logic.
12. In hot markets, still check whether emotional premium is near exhaustion.
13. Ask who is likely paying you before entering.
14. If the likely counterparty is stronger than you, skip the trade.
15. If the market rejects your thesis, correct quickly.
16. Do not defend the identity of being bullish or bearish.
17. After a large loss, stop trading normally for several days.
18. Restart after a large loss with `5%` or smaller size.
19. Require two small correct trades before restoring size.
20. Review every sell-fly and missed trade as cognition feedback, not as a reason to chase.

### Beginner Checklist

Before buying:

1. Is market turnover expanding or shrinking?
2. Is money-making effect expanding?
3. Is this stock a public-favorite candidate?
4. Does this trade fit the current environment, or only my old mode?
5. Has this strategy become too crowded?
6. Who is likely selling to me?
7. Who is likely buying after me?
8. Am I trading emotion in a good market or hard logic in a weak market?
9. Can I exit quickly if the market rejects the thesis?
10. Am I entering because of edge, or because I fear missing out?

After a large profit:

1. Did I make money because the cycle was easy?
2. Did I increase size without a new edge?
3. Is buyer exhaustion appearing?
4. Is turnover still supporting the move?
5. Are others now copying the same tactic?

After a large loss:

1. Have I stopped normal trading?
2. Have I identified whether the loss came from cycle, turnover, crowding, opponent, or psychology?
3. Is my restart position `5%` or smaller?
4. Have I completed two correct small trades before restoring size?

### Copyable Vs Non-Copyable

Copyable:

- Cycle classification.
- Turnover trend check.
- Crowding check.
- Public-favorite filter.
- Counterparty question.
- Post-loss cooling period.
- `5%` small-position restart.
- Two-correct-trade confidence rebuild.

Partially copyable:

- Identifying `大众情人`.
- Switching between emotional premium and hard logic.
- Recognizing when a mode is too popular.
- Judging buyer exhaustion after a profit cycle.

Not directly copyable:

- Long-term market sense.
- Fast recognition of hard logic quality.
- The psychological ability to buy panic and sell euphoria.
- The speaker's accumulated experience and personal judgment.

### Common Misuse

1. Using `5%` restart as a reason to trade immediately after a large loss.
2. Treating expanding turnover as automatic permission to buy anything.
3. Calling a stock `大众情人` just because it rose.
4. Assuming a mode is invalid forever after one bad trade.
5. Assuming a mode is valid forever after one profit phase.
6. Confusing hard logic with a long story.
7. Trying to harvest stronger traders without a real edge.
8. Treating board trading as mechanical chasing rather than consensus confirmation.
9. Using "market is evolving" as an excuse to constantly change systems without evidence.
10. Filling in the missing `18` items without transcript support.

### Missing Details

The source does not clearly define:

1. Exact market-wide turnover thresholds.
2. Exact criteria for `hard logic`.
3. Exact stop-loss percentage after thesis rejection.
4. How to measure strategy crowding before failure.
5. Quantitative definition of `大众情人`.
6. Whether the post-loss cooling period is natural days or trading days.
7. What counts as the two successful restart trades.
8. Standard position sizing outside the post-loss recovery process.
9. How to distinguish buyer exhaustion from normal intraday shakeout.
10. The missing three items implied by the title's `18条忠告`.

## 27. Trader Probability Discipline Framework

- Source file: `/Users/cuiyong/.codex/skills/youtube-investor-distiller/references/trader-probability-discipline-framework.md`

## Trader Probability Discipline Framework

This reference distills the YouTube video `需要经历多少艰辛，才能从亏损累累到稳定盈利？`.

- URL: `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LOqEHRKE21s`
- Channel: `A股作手`
- Main speaker / core figure: a trader describing the path from loss to stable profitability, per NotebookLM transcript.
- Source access: imported into NotebookLM on 2026-06-19 via the user's logged-in browser state.
- NotebookLM limitation: the analysis is based on the YouTube text transcript imported by NotebookLM, not independent audio/video inspection.

This is an educational trading-method note, not investment advice.

### One-Sentence Thesis

稳定盈利不是预测市场，而是用一套正期望系统反复执行：通过`胜率`、`赔率/盈亏比`、`破产率`、`频率`和`仓位`建立可重复规则，再用纪律保证交易者在连亏、诱惑和波动中不破坏系统。

### Highest-Value Principles

1. 没有人能稳定准确预测市场。
   - 专业化的起点是承认市场结果是概率分布，而不是个人判断的必然兑现。
   - Prediction fantasy keeps beginners stuck in emotional trading.

2. 利润是计划执行的副产品。
   - 交易对错不由单笔盈亏决定，而由是否执行系统决定。
   - 盯着账户波动会让人盘中改计划。

3. 赔率比胜率更根本。
   - 低胜率系统也可能赚钱 if payoff is large enough.
   - High win rate with poor payoff can still lose money.

4. 生存第一。
   - 亏损 50% 需要 100% 才能回本。
   - One oversized loss can destroy many correct small trades.

5. 交易收益是非线性的。
   - A good system can have clusters of losses and sudden gains.
   - Traders fail when they abandon the system during normal drawdown.

6. 技术指标不是答案。
   - Indicators can be lagging, contradictory, or ineffective in the wrong cycle.
   - The real upgrade is from indicator worship to probability and system design.

7. 破产率由仓位控制。
   - Even with positive expectancy, oversized bets can lead to ruin.
   - Position size is the gate between a good idea and account survival.

8. 纪律 is the operating system.
   - Discipline connects win rate, payoff, frequency, and position sizing into repeatable behavior.
   - Without discipline, even a good method becomes random.

9. 错过计划内机会也是一种系统亏损。
   - Profits and losses come from the same setup family.
   - Skipping valid winners while taking valid losers destroys expectancy.

10. 盘中不改计划。
    - Analysis and repair belong after market close.
    - Intraday emotional adjustment is often disguised rule-breaking.

11. 良性循环 starts with self-control.
    - Self-discipline creates better execution.
    - Better execution creates confidence.
    - Confidence reinforces self-discipline.

### Four Trader Development Stages

#### Stage 1: 无知小白

Typical behavior:

- Full-position optimism.
- No risk awareness.
- Confuses bull-market luck with skill.
- Learns through large losses.

Upgrade condition:

- Experience enough pain to respect the market.
- Stop believing that confidence equals ability.
- Begin recording trades and risks.

Manual rule:

- Before learning entries, learn how to avoid account-ending behavior.

#### Stage 2: 技术指标迷信

Typical behavior:

- Worships formulas, indicators, and complex mathematics.
- Believes a perfect indicator can predict price.
- Gets confused when indicators lag, conflict, or fail during strong trends.

Breakthrough:

- Understand that indicators are not truth; they are only one way to describe past price and volume.
- Move from prediction to probability.

Upgrade condition:

- Replace "will it rise" with "what is the probability, payoff, invalidation, and sample size?"

#### Stage 3: 概率 / 盈亏比阶段

Typical behavior:

- Starts thinking in win rate and payoff.
- Still struggles with position size and drawdown.
- Can miss valid trades because of caution after losses.

Breakthrough:

- Understand `三率`: win rate, payoff ratio, and ruin risk.
- Realize that system value appears only over a large sample.

Upgrade condition:

- Define a positive-expectancy setup and execute enough repetitions without destroying position rules.

#### Stage 4: 纪律 / 心态阶段

Typical behavior:

- The trader knows the rules but is tested by boredom, greed, fear, and streaks.
- Main risk is not ignorance but self-sabotage.

Breakthrough:

- Judge trades by rule execution rather than single-trade result.
- Use discipline to turn unstable psychology into manageable procedure.

Upgrade condition:

- Mechanical execution through normal wins, losses, missed trades, and temptation.

### Trading Math

#### Core Relationship

Expected output depends on:

- `胜率`: how often the setup wins.
- `赔率 / 盈亏比`: average win relative to average loss.
- `频率`: how often valid opportunities appear.
- `仓位`: how much capital is risked per trade.
- `破产率`: probability that adverse streaks or large losses destroy the account.

Simplified manual formula:

`System value = frequency × (win rate × average win - loss rate × average loss)`, adjusted by position size and ruin risk.

Do not use the transcript's simplified formula mechanically without defining units. The practical lesson is:

- Positive expectancy requires both win probability and payoff.
- Position sizing controls whether the system survives long enough to realize expectancy.

#### Win Rate

Use win rate for:

- Psychological comfort.
- Setup quality tracking.
- Detecting when a strategy deteriorates.

Do not use win rate for:

- Justifying tiny profits and large losses.
- Calling a trade safe enough to overload size.

#### Payoff / 盈亏比

Use payoff for:

- Deciding whether the risk is worth taking.
- Making low-win-rate systems viable.
- Preventing overtrading small edges.

Manual rule:

- Before entry, write the expected loss and expected reward. If the ratio is not attractive enough for the model, skip.

#### Ruin Risk / 破产率

Ruin risk rises when:

- Position size is too large.
- Losses are correlated.
- The trader increases size after losses.
- Stop-loss is ignored.
- A single trade can damage the account beyond repair.

Manual rule:

- Every trade must answer: if this fails, can I still execute the next 20-50 valid trades?

#### Frequency

Frequency matters because:

- A positive system needs enough repetitions to show its edge.
- Too little frequency makes patience difficult and may create pressure to force trades.
- Too much frequency can dilute quality if the setup is not strict.

Manual rule:

- Trade only valid repetitions. Do not manufacture frequency.

### Trading System

#### Setup Selection

Positive setup:

- Has a defined entry condition.
- Has a defined invalidation point.
- Has an expected payoff larger than expected loss.
- Appears often enough for review.
- Fits the trader's execution ability.

Negative setup:

- Chosen after seeing intraday movement.
- Depends on "this time should work."
- Has no stop or no reward estimate.
- Requires emotional courage beyond the trader's current discipline.

#### Entry

Before entry:

1. What setup is this?
2. What is the historical or reviewed win rate?
3. What is the stop?
4. What is the target or reward zone?
5. What is the planned loss as a percent of total capital?
6. Is this inside the plan made before entry?

Entry rule:

- If the trade cannot be written as an if/then plan, do not enter.

#### Exit

Exit when:

- Invalidation point is hit.
- The setup logic fails.
- The planned holding rule says exit.
- The trader realizes the trade was not planned.

Do not exit only because:

- Current P&L causes fear.
- A random opinion appears.
- Price noise occurs before invalidation.

#### Position Management

Core rule:

- Position is determined by planned loss, not excitement.

Example:

- If total capital risk per trade is capped at 2%, and stop distance is 5%, then position size should be sized so a stop-out loses only 2% of account capital.

Guardrail:

- Do not increase size after consecutive losses.
- If the trader cannot execute stops, reduce size before trading again.

#### Loss Handling

After a loss:

1. Was it a valid system loss?
2. Was the trade outside the plan?
3. Was the stop obeyed?
4. Was position size correct?
5. Did I feel revenge or urgency?

Rules:

- A valid loss is system cost.
- An invalid loss is behavior debt.
- Do not repair behavior debt by taking more risk.

#### Review

Review should happen after market close, not during emotional action.

Track:

- Planned versus unplanned trades.
- Win rate by setup.
- Average win / average loss.
- Maximum drawdown.
- Rule-breaking frequency.
- Missed valid trades.
- Position-size errors.

### Copyable Rules

1. `不预测，只执行正期望。`
   - Market prediction is not required; system expectancy is required.

2. `利润是副产品。`
   - Focus on plan quality and execution.

3. `先算亏多少，再想赚多少。`
   - Survival comes before target.

4. `赔率不够，不做。`
   - Do not trade low-payoff setups merely because they look likely.

5. `仓位由止损距离决定。`
   - Size should be reverse-engineered from acceptable account loss.

6. `盘中不改计划。`
   - If the plan needs revision, write it after close and apply next time.

7. `连亏不翻倍。`
   - Loss streaks are where ruin risk explodes.

8. `错过系统内机会也要复盘。`
   - Missing valid winners damages expectancy.

9. `指标只是描述，不是答案。`
   - Use indicators only inside a tested structure.

10. `先活下来，再谈稳定盈利。`
    - Account survival is the platform for compounding.

### Beginner Checklist

Before buying:

1. Is this a named setup from my system?
2. What is the stop?
3. What is the expected reward?
4. Is reward at least 2:1 or 3:1 relative to risk, if that is my model?
5. What percent of my account is lost if stop triggers?
6. Does this trade violate today's plan?
7. Am I entering because of signal or emotion?
8. If I lose, will I still be calm enough for the next valid trade?

After trading:

1. Was this a valid win/loss or a rule-break win/loss?
2. Did I move the stop?
3. Did I change size because of emotion?
4. Did I skip a valid setup because of fear?
5. Did I trade to recover money or to execute a system?

### Common Misuses

- Treating probability thinking as permission to be vague.
- Claiming "positive expectancy" without tracking data.
- Raising size because a setup feels obvious.
- Using a fixed 2% risk without considering volatility and stop distance.
- Cutting winners too early and letting losers reach full stop, destroying payoff.
- After several losses, switching to a different untested setup.
- Mistaking indicator complexity for system robustness.
- Ignoring missed valid trades in review.
- Judging a trade by profit instead of execution quality.
- Believing discipline means stubbornly holding when invalidation has happened.

### Copyable vs Hard To Copy

Copyable:

- Four-stage development map.
- Trade plan template.
- Win-rate and payoff tracking.
- Account-risk-based position sizing.
- Planned stop rules.
- Post-close review process.
- Missed-valid-trade review.

Hard to copy:

- Emotional neutrality after repeated losses.
- Patience during low-opportunity periods.
- Trusting a positive system through normal drawdown.
- Knowing when a system's edge has genuinely decayed versus random variance.
- Boredom tolerance for mechanical repetition.

### Relationship To Existing Frameworks

#### Liangwange Discipline And Equity Curve

Reinforced:

- Discipline is the floor.
- Losses and missed opportunities are normal costs.
- Equity curve should validate size.
- No averaging down or revenge trading.

Added:

- More explicit three-rate math: win rate, payoff, ruin risk.
- More precise link between position size and account survival.
- Treating missed valid trades as expectancy damage.

Manual synthesis:

- Use this framework for the math and execution skeleton.
- Use Liangwange for personality fit, equity-curve validation, and capital-scaling permission.

#### Beijing Chaojia

Reinforced:

- Boundaries and risk filters matter more than heroic buying.
- Mechanical selling reduces behavioral damage.
- Position reduction after losses protects psychology.

Added:

- Explicit account-level risk math behind why boundaries matter.
- Plan-first framing for every trade.

#### Chaogu Yangjia

Reinforced:

- Stable compounding beats stimulation.
- Cost and emotion should not control decisions.
- Heavy size needs exceptional risk/reward.

Difference:

- Yangjia focuses on market emotion and crowd psychology.
- This framework focuses on internal execution math and survival.

Manual synthesis:

- Use Yangjia to judge market opportunity quality.
- Use this framework to decide whether the trade's payoff, size, and discipline are acceptable.

#### Qianniuge Incremental Capital Cycle

Reinforced:

- Cycle context matters; indicators and setups behave differently in trend versus chop.
- Do not trade for recovery.

Difference:

- Qianniuge is top-down: volume cycle, capital source, mainstream sector.
- This framework is bottom-up: trade math, ruin risk, discipline, execution.

Manual synthesis:

- Use Qianniuge to decide whether the environment deserves exposure.
- Use this framework to size and execute the selected trade.

#### Youzi Growth Common Framework

Reinforced:

- Transition from random trading to system execution is a common growth path.
- Plan-first and review-first behavior beat intraday impulse.
- Mainstream and volume matter, but only if the trader can execute rules.

Added:

- Four-stage learning ladder.
- Explicit math behind system expectancy.

### Trading Manual Entries

1. Every trade must be classified as a named setup before entry.
2. Before entry, write stop, expected reward, account-risk percent, and invalidation condition.
3. Position size is computed from acceptable loss, not from confidence.
4. If expected payoff is below the model threshold, skip.
5. If the trade is not in the plan, skip.
6. If invalidation hits, exit. Do not debate.
7. If a loss streak occurs, do not increase size; review whether losses were valid system costs or behavior errors.
8. If a valid setup was missed, record it as expectancy leakage.
9. Review all rule-break wins as dangerous, not as proof of skill.
10. Do not scale capital until both equity curve and execution record show stability.

### Review Questions

1. Did I trade a named setup or improvise?
2. What was the planned stop and reward?
3. What was the account-risk percent?
4. Was the payoff ratio sufficient?
5. Did I follow the plan during the trade?
6. Did I move stops or targets emotionally?
7. Was the result a valid system win/loss or a rule-break win/loss?
8. Did I miss a valid setup due to fear?
9. Did I increase size after losses or excitement?
10. Does my recent equity curve justify current position size?

### Future Distillation Targets

When future videos mention stable profitability, probability, discipline, or trader growth, look for:

- Exact formulas or thresholds for position sizing.
- How the speaker defines acceptable drawdown.
- Concrete examples of valid system loss versus behavior loss.
- How to distinguish strategy decay from normal variance.
- Whether missed trades are reviewed quantitatively.
- How account size changes allowed risk per trade.
